Distracted in school? I don't really remember them saying that, no. I mean, school was a long time ago. I went to this little elementary school, Lincoln Elementary. The building was so old, it had those huge windows that rattled whenever the wind blew hard. And they had these radiators that would hiss and clank all winter long. She had this massive collection of porcelain frogs. They were everywhere—on her desk, on the bookshelves, even lining the windowsills. I always wondered where she got them all. I saw a documentary about the salmon run up there, and the bears just standing in the river catching them right out of the water. It's amazing how nature works like that...

They get this blank stare.\n\nDoctor: During these times when your thoughts and words jump around, do you also feel unusually high or euphoric?\n\nPatient: High? No, no, I don't feel high or euphoric or anything like that. I just feel regular, you know, just trying to get my point across to the team. It's just normal work stuff. Like dealing with the new seasonal displays they just sent us. They shipped these massive cardboard cutouts this year, and they're taking up half the stockroom right now. Which actually reminds me, my own garage is looking exactly like that stockroom lately. I've been meaning to clean it out, maybe have a yard sale this weekend. Yard sales are funny, aren't they? People will stand there and haggle over fifty cents for a used coffee mug. I have this one mug I got on a trip to Seattle back in '98, it has a little chip on the handle, but I still use it every single morning. The coffee just tastes better out of it, you know? My sister lives near Seattle, actually, in this little house with a green roof...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices when no one else is there?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I don't hear anybody talking when they aren't there. Just the regular sounds, you know? Like at work, it's mostly just the store radio. They play the exact same forty songs on a loop every single day. I swear, if I hear that one Phil Collins song again... I actually used to love Phil Collins, though. Saw him live back in, oh, must have been '85? Or '86? We drove my buddy's old Ford Bronco all the way to Chicago for the show. Man, that truck was an absolute beast, drank gas like you wouldn't believe. Gas was so much cheaper back then, too. Now you pull up to the pump and it's like taking out a second mortgage just to get to work. My commute isn't even that bad, maybe twenty minutes, but there's this one traffic light on 4th street that stays red for a solid five minutes. I sit there every morning just staring at the bakery on the corner. They make these blueberry scones...\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm sleeping my usual amount, maybe six or seven hours. I usually try to get to bed right after the late news. The anchors on the local station, they just changed the guy who does the weather. The old guy, Bob, he was there for like twenty years. He always had these terrible puns. The new guy seems a little stiff, you know? He wears these suits that look just a bit too tight in the shoulders. I used to buy suits like that when I first started in retail, thought it made me look sharp. Ended up splitting the seam on a jacket while reaching for a box of winter coats on the top shelf. That was embarrassing. My manager at the time, Brenda, she just laughed and handed me a stapler. Stapled my jacket together for the rest of the shift. Brenda was great, she moved to Florida a few years ago. Somewhere near Tampa, I think. Or maybe it was Orlando? I've never been to Orlando, but I hear the humidity is just brutal down there...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: Plot against me? No, I don't think anyone's out to get me or anything like that. People are mostly just focused on themselves, you know? Like at the store, everyone's just rushing to get their shopping done and get home. Though we did have this one guy last Tuesday who tried to walk right out the front doors with a whole cart full of power drills. The new security sensors went off, and let me tell you, those things are incredibly loud now. Almost gives you a heart attack when they trigger. It actually sounds exactly like the fire alarms at my old college dorm. I lived in this massive brick building, third floor, and they'd run these drills at two in the morning in the dead of winter. We'd all be standing out there in the freezing snow in our pajamas. I had these great flannel pajama pants back then, red and black plaid. You really can't find good quality flannel like that anymore. Everything you buy nowadays just shrinks the second you put it in the dryer. I bought a nice button-down shirt just last month, washed it exactly how the tag said, and now the sleeves don't even reach my wrists...\n\nDoctor: When you were a child in school, did teachers often say you were easily distracted or had trouble staying on topic?\n\nPatient: Distracted in school? I don't really remember them saying that, no. I mean, school was a long time ago. I went to this little elementary school, Lincoln Elementary. The building was so old, it had those huge windows that rattled whenever the wind blew hard. And they had these radiators that would hiss and clank all winter long. It was always either freezing cold or boiling hot in those classrooms. My fourth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable, she used to keep a fan on her desk even in December. She was a real character, Mrs. Gable. She had this massive collection of porcelain frogs. They were everywhere—on her desk, on the bookshelves, even lining the windowsills. I always wondered where she got them all. You know, my aunt had a collection like that, but hers was thimbles. She had thimbles from all over the world. She went on this big cruise to Alaska once and brought back a thimble with a little moose painted on it. I've always wanted to go to Alaska. I saw a documentary about the salmon run up there, and the bears just standing in the river catching them right out of the water. It's amazing how nature works like that...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
More energy? No, I wouldn't say so. Just my normal, everyday energy, I guess. It takes a lot to keep up at the store, especially when they schedule me for the morning shift right after a closing shift. We call those "clopens" in the business. They really take it out of you. Especially lately, because corporate decided to send us all the spring patio furniture early this year. It's still freezing out, and we've got boxes of wicker chairs stacked up to the ceiling in the back room. Wicker is so fragile, too. My grandmother had a whole sunroom full of wicker furniture when I was growing up. It was painted white, and the paint would always chip off if you sat on it wrong. We used to sit out there and drink iced tea in the afternoons. She made the absolute best iced tea, always put a little sprig of fresh mint in it from her garden. She had this massive garden out back, grew tomatoes the size of softballs. You really just can't buy tomatoes like that at the grocery store anymore. They always look perfect and red on the shelf, but you bite into them and they just taste like water. I bought a pack of those hothouse tomatoes last week for a sandwich, and it completely ruined the BLT I was making. The bacon was good, though. Thick cut, applewood smoked. I get it from the butcher down on 4th street, the guy with the big handlebar mustache...

Speaking of my boss, he always wears these terrible ties, like the ones from the 90s with the little geometric shapes. I think he gets them at thrift stores, which is fine, I like thrift stores too, found a great lamp at one last week... but anyway, people just look at me like they can't follow what I'm saying. They get this blank stare.\n\nDoctor: During these times when your thoughts and words jump around, do you also feel unusually high or euphoric?\n\nPatient: High? No, no, I don't feel high or euphoric or anything like that. I just feel regular, you know, just trying to get my point across to the team. It's just normal work stuff. Like dealing with the new seasonal displays they just sent us. They shipped these massive cardboard cutouts this year, and they're taking up half the stockroom right now. Which actually reminds me, my own garage is looking exactly like that stockroom lately. I've been meaning to clean it out, maybe have a yard sale this weekend. Yard sales are funny, aren't they? People will stand there and haggle over fifty cents for a used coffee mug. I have this one mug I got on a trip to Seattle back in '98, it has a little chip on the handle, but I still use it every single morning. The coffee just tastes better out of it, you know? My sister lives near Seattle, actually, in this little house with a green roof...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices when no one else is there?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I don't hear anybody talking when they aren't there. Just the regular sounds, you know? Like at work, it's mostly just the store radio. They play the exact same forty songs on a loop every single day. I swear, if I hear that one Phil Collins song again... I actually used to love Phil Collins, though. Saw him live back in, oh, must have been '85? Or '86? We drove my buddy's old Ford Bronco all the way to Chicago for the show. Man, that truck was an absolute beast, drank gas like you wouldn't believe. Gas was so much cheaper back then, too. Now you pull up to the pump and it's like taking out a second mortgage just to get to work. My commute isn't even that bad, maybe twenty minutes, but there's this one traffic light on 4th street that stays red for a solid five minutes. I sit there every morning just staring at the bakery on the corner. They make these blueberry scones...\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm sleeping my usual amount, maybe six or seven hours. I usually try to get to bed right after the late news. The anchors on the local station, they just changed the guy who does the weather. The old guy, Bob, he was there for like twenty years. He always had these terrible puns. The new guy seems a little stiff, you know? He wears these suits that look just a bit too tight in the shoulders. I used to buy suits like that when I first started in retail, thought it made me look sharp. Ended up splitting the seam on a jacket while reaching for a box of winter coats on the top shelf. That was embarrassing. My manager at the time, Brenda, she just laughed and handed me a stapler. Stapled my jacket together for the rest of the shift. Brenda was great, she moved to Florida a few years ago. Somewhere near Tampa, I think. Or maybe it was Orlando? I've never been to Orlando, but I hear the humidity is just brutal down there...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: Plot against me? No, I don't think anyone's out to get me or anything like that. People are mostly just focused on themselves, you know? Like at the store, everyone's just rushing to get their shopping done and get home. Though we did have this one guy last Tuesday who tried to walk right out the front doors with a whole cart full of power drills. The new security sensors went off, and let me tell you, those things are incredibly loud now. Almost gives you a heart attack when they trigger. It actually sounds exactly like the fire alarms at my old college dorm. I lived in this massive brick building, third floor, and they'd run these drills at two in the morning in the dead of winter. We'd all be standing out there in the freezing snow in our pajamas. I had these great flannel pajama pants back then, red and black plaid. You really can't find good quality flannel like that anymore. Everything you buy nowadays just shrinks the second you put it in the dryer. I bought a nice button-down shirt just last month, washed it exactly how the tag said, and now the sleeves don't even reach my wrists...\n\nDoctor: When you were a child in school, did teachers often say you were easily distracted or had trouble staying on topic?\n\nPatient: Distracted in school? I don't really remember them saying that, no. I mean, school was a long time ago. I went to this little elementary school, Lincoln Elementary. The building was so old, it had those huge windows that rattled whenever the wind blew hard. And they had these radiators that would hiss and clank all winter long. It was always either freezing cold or boiling hot in those classrooms. My fourth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable, she used to keep a fan on her desk even in December. She was a real character, Mrs. Gable. She had this massive collection of porcelain frogs. They were everywhere—on her desk, on the bookshelves, even lining the windowsills. I always wondered where she got them all. You know, my aunt had a collection like that, but hers was thimbles. She had thimbles from all over the world. She went on this big cruise to Alaska once and brought back a thimble with a little moose painted on it. I've always wanted to go to Alaska. I saw a documentary about the salmon run up there, and the bears just standing in the river catching them right out of the water. It's amazing how nature works like that...\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you had much more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: More energy? No, I wouldn't say so. Just my normal, everyday energy, I guess. It takes a lot to keep up at the store, especially when they schedule me for the morning shift right after a closing shift. We call those \"clopens\" in the business. They really take it out of you. Especially lately, because corporate decided to send us all the spring patio furniture early this year. It's still freezing out, and we've got boxes of wicker chairs stacked up to the ceiling in the back room. Wicker is so fragile, too. My grandmother had a whole sunroom full of wicker furniture when I was growing up. It was painted white, and the paint would always chip off if you sat on it wrong. We used to sit out there and drink iced tea in the afternoons. She made the absolute best iced tea, always put a little sprig of fresh mint in it from her garden. She had this massive garden out back, grew tomatoes the size of softballs. You really just can't buy tomatoes like that at the grocery store anymore. They always look perfect and red on the shelf, but you bite into them and they just taste like water. I bought a pack of those hothouse tomatoes last week for a sandwich, and it completely ruined the BLT I was making. The bacon was good, though. Thick cut, applewood smoked. I get it from the butcher down on 4th street, the guy with the big handlebar mustache...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Emotionally flat? Less motivation? I don't... I don't really think so. I mean, I still get up and go to work every day. It's just that the mornings are so dark right now, you know? The sun doesn't even come up until I'm already halfway through my commute. I take the bus, the 42 line. It's usually pretty empty that early, just me and this one lady who always knits. She knits these incredibly intricate sweaters, all cables and different colored yarns. I tried knitting once, back in my twenties. I thought it would be a relaxing hobby, but I could never get the tension right. My stitches were either way too tight or so loose they'd fall right off the needles. Needles are funny things, aren't they? They make those bamboo ones now, which seem nice, but I had the metal ones. They clacked together so loudly. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's wind chimes. He has these huge metal pipes hanging on his front porch, and whenever there's a storm, it's just this constant clanging noise. Last week, we had a storm that knocked out the power for three whole blocks. I had to use a flashlight to find my way around the kitchen. It was one of those heavy, industrial flashlights, the kind that take the big D batteries. They weigh an absolute ton...

She knits these incredibly intricate sweaters, all cables and different colored yarns. I tried knitting once, back in my twenties. I thought it would be a relaxing hobby, but I could never get the tension right. My stitches were either way too tight or so loose they'd fall right off the needles. Needles are funny things, aren't they? They make those bamboo ones now, which seem nice, but I had the metal ones. They clacked together so loudly. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's wind chimes. He has these huge metal pipes hanging on his front porch, and whenever there's a storm, it's just this constant clanging noise. Irritable? No, I wouldn't say unusually irritable. I mean, things get on my nerves at work sometimes, but that's just retail. Like yesterday, someone left a half-empty coffee cup right on top of a stack of the new spring sweaters we just put out. It left a ring on the display table. Those tables are made of this cheap particle board, you know, with the fake wood veneer on top. If water gets under the veneer, it bubbles up and looks terrible. I tried to fix a coffee table like that once in my old apartment. I bought all this wood glue and clamps from the hardware store. The guy at the hardware store was so helpful, he showed me exactly what to get. He was wearing this bright orange apron with all these pockets. I always thought it would be handy to have an apron like that for gardening. My neighbor has one, actually. He grows the most incredible zucchini. He brought me over a loaf of zucchini bread last week. It was still warm from the oven. Baking bread from scratch seems like such a lot of work, all that kneading and waiting for the dough to rise. You have to get the water temperature just right for the yeast, or it won't activate. I remember doing a science experiment with yeast in middle school... we put balloons over these glass bottles to see them inflate. Balloons always remind me of the county fair. They used to have this guy making balloon animals, but he could only ever figure out how to make a dog or a sword.

No, no, I don't hear anybody talking when they aren't there. Just the regular sounds, you know? Like at work, it's mostly just the store radio. They play the exact same forty songs on a loop every single day. I swear, if I hear that one Phil Collins song again... I actually used to love Phil Collins, though. Saw him live back in, oh, must have been '85? Or '86? We drove my buddy's old Ford Bronco all the way to Chicago for the show. Man, that truck was an absolute beast, drank gas like you wouldn't believe. Gas was so much cheaper back then, too. Now you pull up to the pump and it's like taking out a second mortgage just to get to work. My commute isn't even that bad, maybe twenty minutes, but there's this one traffic light on 4th street that stays red for a solid five minutes. I sit there every morning just staring at the bakery on the corner. They make these blueberry scones...\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm sleeping my usual amount, maybe six or seven hours. I usually try to get to bed right after the late news. The anchors on the local station, they just changed the guy who does the weather. The old guy, Bob, he was there for like twenty years. He always had these terrible puns. The new guy seems a little stiff, you know? He wears these suits that look just a bit too tight in the shoulders. I used to buy suits like that when I first started in retail, thought it made me look sharp. Ended up splitting the seam on a jacket while reaching for a box of winter coats on the top shelf. That was embarrassing. My manager at the time, Brenda, she just laughed and handed me a stapler. Stapled my jacket together for the rest of the shift. Brenda was great, she moved to Florida a few years ago. Somewhere near Tampa, I think. Or maybe it was Orlando? I've never been to Orlando, but I hear the humidity is just brutal down there...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: Plot against me? No, I don't think anyone's out to get me or anything like that. People are mostly just focused on themselves, you know? Like at the store, everyone's just rushing to get their shopping done and get home. Though we did have this one guy last Tuesday who tried to walk right out the front doors with a whole cart full of power drills. The new security sensors went off, and let me tell you, those things are incredibly loud now. Almost gives you a heart attack when they trigger. It actually sounds exactly like the fire alarms at my old college dorm. I lived in this massive brick building, third floor, and they'd run these drills at two in the morning in the dead of winter. We'd all be standing out there in the freezing snow in our pajamas. I had these great flannel pajama pants back then, red and black plaid. You really can't find good quality flannel like that anymore. Everything you buy nowadays just shrinks the second you put it in the dryer. I bought a nice button-down shirt just last month, washed it exactly how the tag said, and now the sleeves don't even reach my wrists...\n\nDoctor: When you were a child in school, did teachers often say you were easily distracted or had trouble staying on topic?\n\nPatient: Distracted in school? I don't really remember them saying that, no. I mean, school was a long time ago. I went to this little elementary school, Lincoln Elementary. The building was so old, it had those huge windows that rattled whenever the wind blew hard. And they had these radiators that would hiss and clank all winter long. It was always either freezing cold or boiling hot in those classrooms. My fourth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable, she used to keep a fan on her desk even in December. She was a real character, Mrs. Gable. She had this massive collection of porcelain frogs. They were everywhere—on her desk, on the bookshelves, even lining the windowsills. I always wondered where she got them all. You know, my aunt had a collection like that, but hers was thimbles. She had thimbles from all over the world. She went on this big cruise to Alaska once and brought back a thimble with a little moose painted on it. I've always wanted to go to Alaska. I saw a documentary about the salmon run up there, and the bears just standing in the river catching them right out of the water. It's amazing how nature works like that...\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you had much more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: More energy? No, I wouldn't say so. Just my normal, everyday energy, I guess. It takes a lot to keep up at the store, especially when they schedule me for the morning shift right after a closing shift. We call those \"clopens\" in the business. They really take it out of you. Especially lately, because corporate decided to send us all the spring patio furniture early this year. It's still freezing out, and we've got boxes of wicker chairs stacked up to the ceiling in the back room. Wicker is so fragile, too. My grandmother had a whole sunroom full of wicker furniture when I was growing up. It was painted white, and the paint would always chip off if you sat on it wrong. We used to sit out there and drink iced tea in the afternoons. She made the absolute best iced tea, always put a little sprig of fresh mint in it from her garden. She had this massive garden out back, grew tomatoes the size of softballs. You really just can't buy tomatoes like that at the grocery store anymore. They always look perfect and red on the shelf, but you bite into them and they just taste like water. I bought a pack of those hothouse tomatoes last week for a sandwich, and it completely ruined the BLT I was making. The bacon was good, though. Thick cut, applewood smoked. I get it from the butcher down on 4th street, the guy with the big handlebar mustache...\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling emotionally flat or having much less motivation than usual?\n\nPatient: Emotionally flat? Less motivation? I don't... I don't really think so. I mean, I still get up and go to work every day. It's just that the mornings are so dark right now, you know? The sun doesn't even come up until I'm already halfway through my commute. I take the bus, the 42 line. It's usually pretty empty that early, just me and this one lady who always knits. She knits these incredibly intricate sweaters, all cables and different colored yarns. I tried knitting once, back in my twenties. I thought it would be a relaxing hobby, but I could never get the tension right. My stitches were either way too tight or so loose they'd fall right off the needles. Needles are funny things, aren't they? They make those bamboo ones now, which seem nice, but I had the metal ones. They clacked together so loudly. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's wind chimes. He has these huge metal pipes hanging on his front porch, and whenever there's a storm, it's just this constant clanging noise. Last week, we had a storm that knocked out the power for three whole blocks. I had to use a flashlight to find my way around the kitchen. It was one of those heavy, industrial flashlights, the kind that take the big D batteries. They weigh an absolute ton...\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Irritable? No, I wouldn't say unusually irritable. I mean, things get on my nerves at work sometimes, but that's just retail. Like yesterday, someone left a half-empty coffee cup right on top of a stack of the new spring sweaters we just put out. It left a ring on the display table. Those tables are made of this cheap particle board, you know, with the fake wood veneer on top. If water gets under the veneer, it bubbles up and looks terrible. I tried to fix a coffee table like that once in my old apartment. I bought all this wood glue and clamps from the hardware store. The guy at the hardware store was so helpful, he showed me exactly what to get. He was wearing this bright orange apron with all these pockets. I always thought it would be handy to have an apron like that for gardening. My neighbor has one, actually. He grows the most incredible zucchini. He brought me over a loaf of zucchini bread last week. It was still warm from the oven. Baking bread from scratch seems like such a lot of work, all that kneading and waiting for the dough to rise. You have to get the water temperature just right for the yeast, or it won't activate. I remember doing a science experiment with yeast in middle school... we put balloons over these glass bottles to see them inflate. Balloons always remind me of the county fair. They used to have this guy making balloon animals, but he could only ever figure out how to make a dog or a sword.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

The sun doesn't even come up until I'm already halfway through my commute. I take the bus, the 42 line. It's usually pretty empty that early, just me and this one lady who always knits. She knits these incredibly intricate sweaters, all cables and different colored yarns. I tried knitting once, back in my twenties. I thought it would be a relaxing hobby, but I could never get the tension right. My stitches were either way too tight or so loose they'd fall right off the needles. Needles are funny things, aren't they? They make those bamboo ones now, which seem nice, but I had the metal ones. They clacked together so loudly. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's wind chimes. He has these huge metal pipes hanging on his front porch, and whenever there's a storm, it's just this constant clanging noise. Last week, we had a storm that knocked out the power for three whole blocks. I had to use a flashlight to find my way around the kitchen. It was one of those heavy, industrial flashlights, the kind that take the big D batteries. They weigh an absolute ton...\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Irritable? No, I wouldn't say unusually irritable. I mean, things get on my nerves at work sometimes, but that's just retail. Like yesterday, someone left a half-empty coffee cup right on top of a stack of the new spring sweaters we just put out. It left a ring on the display table. Those tables are made of this cheap particle board, you know, with the fake wood veneer on top. If water gets under the veneer, it bubbles up and looks terrible. I tried to fix a coffee table like that once in my old apartment. I bought all this wood glue and clamps from the hardware store. The guy at the hardware store was so helpful, he showed me exactly what to get. He was wearing this bright orange apron with all these pockets. I always thought it would be handy to have an apron like that for gardening. My neighbor has one, actually. He grows the most incredible zucchini. He brought me over a loaf of zucchini bread last week. It was still warm from the oven. Baking bread from scratch seems like such a lot of work, all that kneading and waiting for the dough to rise. You have to get the water temperature just right for the yeast, or it won't activate. I remember doing a science experiment with yeast in middle school... we put balloons over these glass bottles to see them inflate. Balloons always remind me of the county fair. They used to have this guy making balloon animals, but he could only ever figure out how to make a dog or a sword.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had a period when you felt very depressed?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 59 / first_line_supervisor_of_retail_sales_worker / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Depressed? No, I haven't really had anything like that. I mean, sure, you get a little down when the weather is miserable, but not depressed. It's been raining so much lately, just this constant, freezing drizzle. There are puddles everywhere downtown. I completely ruined a good pair of loafers stepping off the curb yesterday. They're these brown leather slip-ons, really comfortable for standing on the sales floor all day, but leather and water just do not mix. You have to let them dry out naturally, you know. You can't put them near a radiator or the leather cracks. I learned that the hard way with a baseball glove when I was a kid. I left it right on top of the heating grate in the hallway to dry out. My dad was so mad, he had spent a good chunk of change on that glove. It was a Rawlings, smelled amazing when it was brand new. There's nothing quite like the smell of new leather, is there? Kind of like that new car smell. I haven't bought a new car in... gosh, must be fifteen years now. It's a Honda, runs like a top, but the upholstery is starting to fray on the driver's side seat. I tried putting one of those wooden beaded seat covers over it, the ones cab drivers use, but they just end up pinching your back when you take a sharp turn...

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because... at work, you know, I manage the retail floor, and when I try to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule to the team, my words just start jumping. I'll be talking about the delivery trucks, and then suddenly it's about Thursdays, and then I'm thinking about the church grill we used last summer, which reminds me of my boss's tone of voice when he's annoyed. Speaking of my boss, he always wears these terrible ties, like the ones from the 90s with the little geometric shapes. I think he gets them at thrift stores, which is fine, I like thrift stores too, found a great lamp at one last week... but anyway, people just look at me like they can't follow what I'm saying. No, no, I don't feel high or euphoric or anything like that. I just feel regular, you know, just trying to get my point across to the team. It's just normal work stuff. Like dealing with the new seasonal displays they just sent us. They shipped these massive cardboard cutouts this year, and they're taking up half the stockroom right now. People will stand there and haggle over fifty cents for a used coffee mug. I have this one mug I got on a trip to Seattle back in '98, it has a little chip on the handle, but I still use it every single morning. The coffee just tastes better out of it, you know? My sister lives near Seattle, actually, in this little house with a green roof...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices when no one else is there?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I don't hear anybody talking when they aren't there. Just the regular sounds, you know? Like at work, it's mostly just the store radio. They play the exact same forty songs on a loop every single day. I swear, if I hear that one Phil Collins song again... I actually used to love Phil Collins, though. Saw him live back in, oh, must have been '85? Or '86? We drove my buddy's old Ford Bronco all the way to Chicago for the show. Man, that truck was an absolute beast, drank gas like you wouldn't believe. Gas was so much cheaper back then, too. Now you pull up to the pump and it's like taking out a second mortgage just to get to work. My commute isn't even that bad, maybe twenty minutes, but there's this one traffic light on 4th street that stays red for a solid five minutes. I sit there every morning just staring at the bakery on the corner. They make these blueberry scones...\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm sleeping my usual amount, maybe six or seven hours. I usually try to get to bed right after the late news. The anchors on the local station, they just changed the guy who does the weather. The old guy, Bob, he was there for like twenty years. He always had these terrible puns. The new guy seems a little stiff, you know? He wears these suits that look just a bit too tight in the shoulders. I used to buy suits like that when I first started in retail, thought it made me look sharp. Ended up splitting the seam on a jacket while reaching for a box of winter coats on the top shelf. That was embarrassing. My manager at the time, Brenda, she just laughed and handed me a stapler. Stapled my jacket together for the rest of the shift. Brenda was great, she moved to Florida a few years ago. Somewhere near Tampa, I think. Or maybe it was Orlando? I've never been to Orlando, but I hear the humidity is just brutal down there...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: Plot against me? No, I don't think anyone's out to get me or anything like that. People are mostly just focused on themselves, you know? Like at the store, everyone's just rushing to get their shopping done and get home. Though we did have this one guy last Tuesday who tried to walk right out the front doors with a whole cart full of power drills. The new security sensors went off, and let me tell you, those things are incredibly loud now. Almost gives you a heart attack when they trigger. It actually sounds exactly like the fire alarms at my old college dorm. I lived in this massive brick building, third floor, and they'd run these drills at two in the morning in the dead of winter. We'd all be standing out there in the freezing snow in our pajamas. I had these great flannel pajama pants back then, red and black plaid. You really can't find good quality flannel like that anymore. Everything you buy nowadays just shrinks the second you put it in the dryer. I bought a nice button-down shirt just last month, washed it exactly how the tag said, and now the sleeves don't even reach my wrists...\n\nDoctor: When you were a child in school, did teachers often say you were easily distracted or had trouble staying on topic?\n\nPatient: Distracted in school? I don't really remember them saying that, no. I mean, school was a long time ago. I went to this little elementary school, Lincoln Elementary. The building was so old, it had those huge windows that rattled whenever the wind blew hard. And they had these radiators that would hiss and clank all winter long. It was always either freezing cold or boiling hot in those classrooms. My fourth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable, she used to keep a fan on her desk even in December. She was a real character, Mrs. Gable. She had this massive collection of porcelain frogs. They were everywhere—on her desk, on the bookshelves, even lining the windowsills. I always wondered where she got them all. You know, my aunt had a collection like that, but hers was thimbles. She had thimbles from all over the world. She went on this big cruise to Alaska once and brought back a thimble with a little moose painted on it. I've always wanted to go to Alaska. I saw a documentary about the salmon run up there, and the bears just standing in the river catching them right out of the water. It's amazing how nature works like that...\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you had much more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: More energy? No, I wouldn't say so. Just my normal, everyday energy, I guess. It takes a lot to keep up at the store, especially when they schedule me for the morning shift right after a closing shift. We call those \"clopens\" in the business. They really take it out of you. Especially lately, because corporate decided to send us all the spring patio furniture early this year. It's still freezing out, and we've got boxes of wicker chairs stacked up to the ceiling in the back room. Wicker is so fragile, too. My grandmother had a whole sunroom full of wicker furniture when I was growing up. It was painted white, and the paint would always chip off if you sat on it wrong. We used to sit out there and drink iced tea in the afternoons. She made the absolute best iced tea, always put a little sprig of fresh mint in it from her garden. She had this massive garden out back, grew tomatoes the size of softballs. You really just can't buy tomatoes like that at the grocery store anymore. They always look perfect and red on the shelf, but you bite into them and they just taste like water. I bought a pack of those hothouse tomatoes last week for a sandwich, and it completely ruined the BLT I was making. The bacon was good, though. Thick cut, applewood smoked. I get it from the butcher down on 4th street, the guy with the big handlebar mustache...\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling emotionally flat or having much less motivation than usual?\n\nPatient: Emotionally flat? Episodes? No, I wouldn't say it's like that. It's just... it's been building for a while, you know? Like, maybe 11 months or so. It really started when the new district manager came in. You know, my aunt had a collection like that, but hers was thimbles. She had thimbles from all over the world. She knits these incredibly intricate sweaters, all cables and different colored yarns. I tried knitting once, back in my twenties. I thought it would be a relaxing hobby, but I could never get the tension right. My stitches were either way too tight or so loose they'd fall right off the needles. Needles are funny things, aren't they? They make those bamboo ones now, which seem nice, but I had the metal ones. They clacked together so loudly. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's wind chimes. He has these huge metal pipes hanging on his front porch, and whenever there's a storm, it's just this constant clanging noise. Well, yeah, I guess my mood has been fine mostly. Normal, you know. But... the jumbled explanations and all those odd stops I make on the sales floor, that's been pretty noticeable for about six months now. The district manager actually pulled me aside about it. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.9, F25.0, F31.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because... at work, you know, I manage the retail floor, and when I try to explain the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule to the team, my words just start jumping. I'll be talking about the delivery trucks, and then suddenly it's about Thursdays, and then I'm thinking about the church grill we used last summer, which reminds me of my boss's tone of voice when he's annoyed. Speaking of my boss, he always wears these terrible ties, like the ones from the 90s with the little geometric shapes. I think he gets them at thrift stores, which is fine, I like thrift stores too, found a great lamp at one last week... but anyway, people just look at me like they can't follow what I'm saying. They get this blank stare.\n\nDoctor: During these times when your thoughts and words jump around, do you also feel unusually high or euphoric?\n\nPatient: High? No, no, I don't feel high or euphoric or anything like that. I just feel regular, you know, just trying to get my point across to the team. It's just normal work stuff. Like dealing with the new seasonal displays they just sent us. They shipped these massive cardboard cutouts this year, and they're taking up half the stockroom right now. Which actually reminds me, my own garage is looking exactly like that stockroom lately. I've been meaning to clean it out, maybe have a yard sale this weekend. Yard sales are funny, aren't they? People will stand there and haggle over fifty cents for a used coffee mug. I have this one mug I got on a trip to Seattle back in '98, it has a little chip on the handle, but I still use it every single morning. The coffee just tastes better out of it, you know? My sister lives near Seattle, actually, in this little house with a green roof...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear voices when no one else is there?\n\nPatient: Voices? No, no, I don't hear anybody talking when they aren't there. Just the regular sounds, you know? Like at work, it's mostly just the store radio. They play the exact same forty songs on a loop every single day. I swear, if I hear that one Phil Collins song again... I actually used to love Phil Collins, though. Saw him live back in, oh, must have been '85? Or '86? We drove my buddy's old Ford Bronco all the way to Chicago for the show. Man, that truck was an absolute beast, drank gas like you wouldn't believe. Gas was so much cheaper back then, too. Now you pull up to the pump and it's like taking out a second mortgage just to get to work. My commute isn't even that bad, maybe twenty minutes, but there's this one traffic light on 4th street that stays red for a solid five minutes. I sit there every morning just staring at the bakery on the corner. They make these blueberry scones...\n\nDoctor: Have you been needing much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm sleeping my usual amount, maybe six or seven hours. I usually try to get to bed right after the late news. The anchors on the local station, they just changed the guy who does the weather. The old guy, Bob, he was there for like twenty years. He always had these terrible puns. The new guy seems a little stiff, you know? He wears these suits that look just a bit too tight in the shoulders. I used to buy suits like that when I first started in retail, thought it made me look sharp. Ended up splitting the seam on a jacket while reaching for a box of winter coats on the top shelf. That was embarrassing. My manager at the time, Brenda, she just laughed and handed me a stapler. Stapled my jacket together for the rest of the shift. Brenda was great, she moved to Florida a few years ago. Somewhere near Tampa, I think. Or maybe it was Orlando? I've never been to Orlando, but I hear the humidity is just brutal down there...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: Plot against me? No, I don't think anyone's out to get me or anything like that. People are mostly just focused on themselves, you know? Like at the store, everyone's just rushing to get their shopping done and get home. Though we did have this one guy last Tuesday who tried to walk right out the front doors with a whole cart full of power drills. The new security sensors went off, and let me tell you, those things are incredibly loud now. Almost gives you a heart attack when they trigger. It actually sounds exactly like the fire alarms at my old college dorm. I lived in this massive brick building, third floor, and they'd run these drills at two in the morning in the dead of winter. We'd all be standing out there in the freezing snow in our pajamas. I had these great flannel pajama pants back then, red and black plaid. You really can't find good quality flannel like that anymore. Everything you buy nowadays just shrinks the second you put it in the dryer. I bought a nice button-down shirt just last month, washed it exactly how the tag said, and now the sleeves don't even reach my wrists...\n\nDoctor: When you were a child in school, did teachers often say you were easily distracted or had trouble staying on topic?\n\nPatient: Distracted in school? I don't really remember them saying that, no. I mean, school was a long time ago. I went to this little elementary school, Lincoln Elementary. The building was so old, it had those huge windows that rattled whenever the wind blew hard. And they had these radiators that would hiss and clank all winter long. It was always either freezing cold or boiling hot in those classrooms. My fourth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable, she used to keep a fan on her desk even in December. She was a real character, Mrs. Gable. She had this massive collection of porcelain frogs. They were everywhere—on her desk, on the bookshelves, even lining the windowsills. I always wondered where she got them all. You know, my aunt had a collection like that, but hers was thimbles. She had thimbles from all over the world. She went on this big cruise to Alaska once and brought back a thimble with a little moose painted on it. I've always wanted to go to Alaska. I saw a documentary about the salmon run up there, and the bears just standing in the river catching them right out of the water. It's amazing how nature works like that...\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you had much more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: More energy? No, I wouldn't say so. Just my normal, everyday energy, I guess. It takes a lot to keep up at the store, especially when they schedule me for the morning shift right after a closing shift. We call those \"clopens\" in the business. They really take it out of you. Especially lately, because corporate decided to send us all the spring patio furniture early this year. It's still freezing out, and we've got boxes of wicker chairs stacked up to the ceiling in the back room. Wicker is so fragile, too. My grandmother had a whole sunroom full of wicker furniture when I was growing up. It was painted white, and the paint would always chip off if you sat on it wrong. We used to sit out there and drink iced tea in the afternoons. She made the absolute best iced tea, always put a little sprig of fresh mint in it from her garden. She had this massive garden out back, grew tomatoes the size of softballs. You really just can't buy tomatoes like that at the grocery store anymore. They always look perfect and red on the shelf, but you bite into them and they just taste like water. I bought a pack of those hothouse tomatoes last week for a sandwich, and it completely ruined the BLT I was making. The bacon was good, though. Thick cut, applewood smoked. I get it from the butcher down on 4th street, the guy with the big handlebar mustache...\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself feeling emotionally flat or having much less motivation than usual?\n\nPatient: Emotionally flat? Less motivation? I don't... I don't really think so. I mean, I still get up and go to work every day. It's just that the mornings are so dark right now, you know? The sun doesn't even come up until I'm already halfway through my commute. I take the bus, the 42 line. It's usually pretty empty that early, just me and this one lady who always knits. She knits these incredibly intricate sweaters, all cables and different colored yarns. I tried knitting once, back in my twenties. I thought it would be a relaxing hobby, but I could never get the tension right. My stitches were either way too tight or so loose they'd fall right off the needles. Needles are funny things, aren't they? They make those bamboo ones now, which seem nice, but I had the metal ones. They clacked together so loudly. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's wind chimes. He has these huge metal pipes hanging on his front porch, and whenever there's a storm, it's just this constant clanging noise. Last week, we had a storm that knocked out the power for three whole blocks. I had to use a flashlight to find my way around the kitchen. It was one of those heavy, industrial flashlights, the kind that take the big D batteries. They weigh an absolute ton...\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Irritable? No, I wouldn't say unusually irritable. I mean, things get on my nerves at work sometimes, but that's just retail. Like yesterday, someone left a half-empty coffee cup right on top of a stack of the new spring sweaters we just put out. It left a ring on the display table. Those tables are made of this cheap particle board, you know, with the fake wood veneer on top. If water gets under the veneer, it bubbles up and looks terrible. I tried to fix a coffee table like that once in my old apartment. I bought all this wood glue and clamps from the hardware store. The guy at the hardware store was so helpful, he showed me exactly what to get. He was wearing this bright orange apron with all these pockets. I always thought it would be handy to have an apron like that for gardening. My neighbor has one, actually. He grows the most incredible zucchini. He brought me over a loaf of zucchini bread last week. It was still warm from the oven. Baking bread from scratch seems like such a lot of work, all that kneading and waiting for the dough to rise. You have to get the water temperature just right for the yeast, or it won't activate. I remember doing a science experiment with yeast in middle school... we put balloons over these glass bottles to see them inflate. Balloons always remind me of the county fair. They used to have this guy making balloon animals, but he could only ever figure out how to make a dog or a sword.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you felt very depressed?\n\nPatient: Depressed? No, I haven't really had anything like that. I mean, sure, you get a little down when the weather is miserable, but not depressed. It's been raining so much lately, just this constant, freezing drizzle. There are puddles everywhere downtown. I completely ruined a good pair of loafers stepping off the curb yesterday. They're these brown leather slip-ons, really comfortable for standing on the sales floor all day, but leather and water just do not mix. You have to let them dry out naturally, you know. You can't put them near a radiator or the leather cracks. I learned that the hard way with a baseball glove when I was a kid. I left it right on top of the heating grate in the hallway to dry out. My dad was so mad, he had spent a good chunk of change on that glove. It was a Rawlings, smelled amazing when it was brand new. There's nothing quite like the smell of new leather, is there? Kind of like that new car smell. I haven't bought a new car in... gosh, must be fifteen years now. It's a Honda, runs like a top, but the upholstery is starting to fray on the driver's side seat. I tried putting one of those wooden beaded seat covers over it, the ones cab drivers use, but they just end up pinching your back when you take a sharp turn...\n\nDoctor: Do these problems with your thoughts jumping from topic to topic come in episodes, or are they there all the time?\n\nPatient: Episodes? No, I wouldn't say it's like that. It's just... it's been building for a while, you know? Like, maybe 11 months or so. It really started when the new district manager came in. He started pushing us so hard on all these forecasts and the weekly schedules. Schedules are such a nightmare to write anyway, especially around the holidays when everyone wants time off. Last year, I had three cashiers all request the same weekend off in December to go to this music festival down in the city. I don't understand the appeal of those festivals, to be honest. It's just thousands of people crammed into a muddy field, paying fifty dollars for a bottle of water. I went to a concert once in the seventies, saw Fleetwood Mac. We had lawn seats, and it rained the entire time. We had to use these cheap plastic ponchos we bought at a gas station on the way there. They were bright yellow, the kind that rip the second you try to put your arms through the holes. Gas stations used to sell the weirdest stuff, didn't they? Now it's all just energy drinks and those little phone chargers that break after two days. I bought one of those chargers for my son last Christmas, and the wire frayed right at the base...\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have there been times when your speech was this hard to follow even though your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess my mood has been fine mostly. Normal, you know. But... the jumbled explanations and all those odd stops I make on the sales floor, that's been pretty noticeable for about six months now. The district manager actually pulled me aside about it. We were standing right next to the clearance rack, the one with the red stickers. Red stickers always mean final sale, no returns. People get so angry when they try to return something with a red sticker. They bring it up to the register, and you have to point out the policy printed right there on the receipt. Receipts are so long these days, aren't they? You buy one pack of gum and they hand you a foot of paper with all these surveys and coupons on the back. I used to clip coupons with my grandmother on Sunday mornings. She had this special pair of scissors, really heavy metal ones, that were only for coupons. She kept them in an old cigar box on the kitchen counter. The box smelled like dried tobacco and peppermints. I love the smell of peppermint, especially around the holidays. We used to hang candy canes on the Christmas tree...\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

{"candidates":["F20.9","F25.0"],"note":"Chronic disorganized speech/thought disorder for ~11 months, with clear impairment and no episodes of elevated/irritable mood, decreased need for sleep, increased energy, depression, hallucinations, or delusions. This makes schizophrenia-spectrum illness more likely and argues against bipolar I mania (F31.1). Schizoaffective disorder remains a consideration only if mood episodes later emerge or were underreported.","is_final":false}

[
  {

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Energetic? No, I've never really been the revved-up type. If anything, by the time my shift is over, my feet are killing me and I'm just looking forward to sitting down. You stand on that hard commercial tile all day and it just drains the energy right out of you. The younger kids on the floor, though, they are always revved up. They drink those giant cans of energy drinks in the breakroom. I don't know how their hearts don't just explode out of their chests with all that caffeine and sugar. I stick to regular black coffee, myself. We have this old drip machine in the back office, the kind with the glass carafe that always gets that burnt brown ring at the bottom because someone leaves it on the hot plate all afternoon. I tried bringing in a nice French press once, but nobody wanted to take the time to clean it out. Cleaning out coffee grounds is a mess anyway, they get stuck in the sponge and then you're just smearing grounds all over the next mug you wash. I have this favorite mug at home, it's got a picture of a moose on it from when we went up to Maine years ago. Maine is just beautiful in the fall, the leaves turn these incredible shades of orange and red. We stayed in this little bed and breakfast right on the coast, and every morning you could hear the foghorns from the harbor...

I've never been to Orlando, but I hear the humidity is just brutal down there...\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that people are trying to harm you or plot against you?\n\nPatient: Plot against me? The new security sensors went off, and let me tell you, those things are incredibly loud now. Almost gives you a heart attack when they trigger. It actually sounds exactly like the fire alarms at my old college dorm. I lived in this massive brick building, third floor, and they'd run these drills at two in the morning in the dead of winter. We'd all be standing out there in the freezing snow in our pajamas. I had these great flannel pajama pants back then, red and black plaid. You really can't find good quality flannel like that anymore. Everything you buy nowadays just shrinks the second you put it in the dryer. I bought a nice button-down shirt just last month, washed it exactly how the tag said, and now the sleeves don't even reach my wrists...\n\nDoctor: When you were a child in school, did teachers often say you were easily distracted or had trouble staying on topic?\n\nPatient: Distracted in school? I don't really remember them saying that, no. I mean, school was a long time ago. I went to this little elementary school, Lincoln Elementary. The building was so old, it had those huge windows that rattled whenever the wind blew hard. And they had these radiators that would hiss and clank all winter long. It was always either freezing cold or boiling hot in those classrooms. My fourth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable, she used to keep a fan on her desk even in December. She was a real character, Mrs. Gable. She had this massive collection of porcelain frogs. They were everywhere—on her desk, on the bookshelves, even lining the windowsills. I always wondered where she got them all. You know, my aunt had a collection like that, but hers was thimbles. She had thimbles from all over the world. She went on this big cruise to Alaska once and brought back a thimble with a little moose painted on it. I've always wanted to go to Alaska. I saw a documentary about the salmon run up there, and the bears just standing in the river catching them right out of the water. It's amazing how nature works like that...\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you had much more energy than usual?\n\nPatient: More energy? No, I wouldn't say so. Just my normal, everyday energy, I guess. It takes a lot to keep up at the store, especially when they schedule me for the morning shift right after a closing shift. We call those \"clopens\" in the business. They really take it out of you. Especially lately, because corporate decided to send us all the spring patio furniture early this year. It's still freezing out, and we've got boxes of wicker chairs stacked up to the ceiling in the back room. Wicker is so fragile, too. My grandmother had a whole sunroom full of wicker furniture when I was growing up. It was painted white, and the paint would always chip off if you sat on it wrong. He has these huge metal pipes hanging on his front porch, and whenever there's a storm, it's just this constant clanging noise. The guy at the hardware store was so helpful, he showed me exactly what to get. He was wearing this bright orange apron with all these pockets. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Powerful? No, I wouldn't say that. I'm just a floor supervisor, you know? There's nothing grand about making sure the endcaps are stocked and the registers have enough change. Although, getting change has been a real hassle lately. The bank down the street changed their hours, so now I have to go on my lunch break if we need rolls of quarters. I hate going to that bank. The tellers are always so slow, and they have these velvet ropes set up like it's a movie premiere or something. I remember when going to the movies was an event. You'd dress up a little, get a bucket of popcorn that actually tasted like real butter. Now it's all fake butter flavoring, just yellow oil they pump out of a jug. It leaves this weird film on the roof of your mouth. I tried making popcorn on the stove at home the other night, the old-fashioned way with oil in a pot. I must have had the heat too high because I burnt the whole batch. The smoke detector went off, and my dog, Buster, he absolutely hates that sound. He went hiding under the guest bed and wouldn't come out for two hours. I had to bribe him with a piece of deli turkey. Turkey is getting so expensive at the grocery store...

I don't really remember them saying that, no. I mean, school was a long time ago. I went to this little elementary school, Lincoln Elementary. It was always either freezing cold or boiling hot in those classrooms. My fourth-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable, she used to keep a fan on her desk even in December. She was a real character, Mrs. Gable. People get so angry when they try to return something with a red sticker. They bring it up to the register, and you have to point out the policy printed right there on the receipt. Receipts are so long these days, aren't they? You buy one pack of gum and they hand you a foot of paper with all these surveys and coupons on the back. I used to clip coupons with my grandmother on Sunday mornings. She had this special pair of scissors, really heavy metal ones, that were only for coupons. She kept them in an old cigar box on the kitchen counter. The box smelled like dried tobacco and peppermints. I love the smell of peppermint, especially around the holidays. We used to hang candy canes on the Christmas tree...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt so energetic or revved up that other people said you were acting differently?\n\nPatient: Energetic? Needles are funny things, aren't they? They make those bamboo ones now, which seem nice, but I had the metal ones. They clacked together so loudly. It sounded exactly like my neighbor's wind chimes. He has these huge metal pipes hanging on his front porch, and whenever there's a storm, it's just this constant clanging noise. Last week, we had a storm that knocked out the power for three whole blocks. I had to use a flashlight to find my way around the kitchen. It was one of those heavy, industrial flashlights, the kind that take the big D batteries. They weigh an absolute ton...\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually irritable?\n\nPatient: Irritable? No, I wouldn't say unusually irritable. I mean, things get on my nerves at work sometimes, but that's just retail. Like yesterday, someone left a half-empty coffee cup right on top of a stack of the new spring sweaters we just put out. It left a ring on the display table. Those tables are made of this cheap particle board, you know, with the fake wood veneer on top. If water gets under the veneer, it bubbles up and looks terrible. I tried to fix a coffee table like that once in my old apartment. I bought all this wood glue and clamps from the hardware store. The guy at the hardware store was so helpful, he showed me exactly what to get. He was wearing this bright orange apron with all these pockets. I always thought it would be handy to have an apron like that for gardening. My neighbor has one, actually. He grows the most incredible zucchini. He brought me over a loaf of zucchini bread last week. It was still warm from the oven. Baking bread from scratch seems like such a lot of work, all that kneading and waiting for the dough to rise. You have to get the water temperature just right for the yeast, or it won't activate. I remember doing a science experiment with yeast in middle school... we put balloons over these glass bottles to see them inflate. Balloons always remind me of the county fair. They used to have this guy making balloon animals, but he could only ever figure out how to make a dog or a sword.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period when you felt very depressed?\n\nPatient: Depressed? No, I haven't really had anything like that. I mean, sure, you get a little down when the weather is miserable, but not depressed. It's been raining so much lately, just this constant, freezing drizzle. There are puddles everywhere downtown. I completely ruined a good pair of loafers stepping off the curb yesterday. They're these brown leather slip-ons, really comfortable for standing on the sales floor all day, but leather and water just do not mix. You have to let them dry out naturally, you know. You can't put them near a radiator or the leather cracks. I learned that the hard way with a baseball glove when I was a kid. I left it right on top of the heating grate in the hallway to dry out. My dad was so mad, he had spent a good chunk of change on that glove. It was a Rawlings, smelled amazing when it was brand new. There's nothing quite like the smell of new leather, is there? Kind of like that new car smell. I haven't bought a new car in... gosh, must be fifteen years now. It's a Honda, runs like a top, but the upholstery is starting to fray on the driver's side seat. I tried putting one of those wooden beaded seat covers over it, the ones cab drivers use, but they just end up pinching your back when you take a sharp turn...\n\nDoctor: Do these problems with your thoughts jumping from topic to topic come in episodes, or are they there all the time?\n\nPatient: Episodes? No, I wouldn't say it's like that. It's just... it's been building for a while, you know? Like, maybe 11 months or so. It really started when the new district manager came in. He started pushing us so hard on all these forecasts and the weekly schedules. Schedules are such a nightmare to write anyway, especially around the holidays when everyone wants time off. Last year, I had three cashiers all request the same weekend off in December to go to this music festival down in the city. I don't understand the appeal of those festivals, to be honest. It's just thousands of people crammed into a muddy field, paying fifty dollars for a bottle of water. I went to a concert once in the seventies, saw Fleetwood Mac. We had lawn seats, and it rained the entire time. We had to use these cheap plastic ponchos we bought at a gas station on the way there. They were bright yellow, the kind that rip the second you try to put your arms through the holes. Gas stations used to sell the weirdest stuff, didn't they? Now it's all just energy drinks and those little phone chargers that break after two days. I bought one of those chargers for my son last Christmas, and the wire frayed right at the base...\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have there been times when your speech was this hard to follow even though your mood felt completely normal?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess my mood has been fine mostly. Normal, you know. But... the jumbled explanations and all those odd stops I make on the sales floor, that's been pretty noticeable for about six months now. The district manager actually pulled me aside about it. We were standing right next to the clearance rack, the one with the red stickers. Red stickers always mean final sale, no returns. People get so angry when they try to return something with a red sticker. They bring it up to the register, and you have to point out the policy printed right there on the receipt. Receipts are so long these days, aren't they? You buy one pack of gum and they hand you a foot of paper with all these surveys and coupons on the back. I used to clip coupons with my grandmother on Sunday mornings. She had this special pair of scissors, really heavy metal ones, that were only for coupons. She kept them in an old cigar box on the kitchen counter. The box smelled like dried tobacco and peppermints. I love the smell of peppermint, especially around the holidays. We used to hang candy canes on the Christmas tree...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt so energetic or revved up that other people said you were acting differently?\n\nPatient: Energetic? No, I've never really been the revved-up type. If anything, by the time my shift is over, my feet are killing me and I'm just looking forward to sitting down. You stand on that hard commercial tile all day and it just drains the energy right out of you. The younger kids on the floor, though, they are always revved up. They drink those giant cans of energy drinks in the breakroom. I don't know how their hearts don't just explode out of their chests with all that caffeine and sugar. I stick to regular black coffee, myself. We have this old drip machine in the back office, the kind with the glass carafe that always gets that burnt brown ring at the bottom because someone leaves it on the hot plate all afternoon. I tried bringing in a nice French press once, but nobody wanted to take the time to clean it out. Cleaning out coffee grounds is a mess anyway, they get stuck in the sponge and then you're just smearing grounds all over the next mug you wash. I have this favorite mug at home, it's got a picture of a moose on it from when we went up to Maine years ago. Maine is just beautiful in the fall, the leaves turn these incredible shades of orange and red. We stayed in this little bed and breakfast right on the coast, and every morning you could hear the foghorns from the harbor...\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you felt unusually grand or especially powerful?\n\nPatient: Powerful? No, I wouldn't say that. I'm just a floor supervisor, you know? There's nothing grand about making sure the endcaps are stocked and the registers have enough change. Although, getting change has been a real hassle lately. The bank down the street changed their hours, so now I have to go on my lunch break if we need rolls of quarters. I hate going to that bank. The tellers are always so slow, and they have these velvet ropes set up like it's a movie premiere or something. I remember when going to the movies was an event. You'd dress up a little, get a bucket of popcorn that actually tasted like real butter. Now it's all fake butter flavoring, just yellow oil they pump out of a jug. It leaves this weird film on the roof of your mouth. I tried making popcorn on the stove at home the other night, the old-fashioned way with oil in a pot. I must have had the heat too high because I burnt the whole batch. The smoke detector went off, and my dog, Buster, he absolutely hates that sound. He went hiding under the guest bed and wouldn't come out for two hours. I had to bribe him with a piece of deli turkey. Turkey is getting so expensive at the grocery store..."
  }

Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli. Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.
